## Tank and half-track driving (c_ETTank) (2026-09-16; TANK-3 refuted and both gear curves read 2026-09-19; TANK-1 and TANK-7 refuted, TANK-9 corrected and the gearbox read 2026-09-20)

> **The headline of this section was wrong until 2026-09-20.** "There is no
> tank-specific code path in the engine" came from an exhaustive grep that could
> not see a virtual call (TANK-1). `engineType` is a **bit field** read nine
> times, and bit 0 decides whether `PhysicsEngine::updatePhysics` runs at all —
> so a car and a tank do not merely wire different axes to the same code, they
> take different paths through it. What survives is the *steering* half: the
> differential is ordinary per-wheel friction code (TANK-2), and both a car and
> a tank are propelled by the same EngineGrip contact-speed target (TANK-9),
> which is what makes TANK-7's hull-thrust model wrong at the root.

Settled for the map viewer's `TrackedVehicle` (`ground.js`). Full narrative:
[subsystems/tank-driving.md](subsystems/tank-driving.md).

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| TANK-1 | ~~`engineType` is parsed and dumped back by `makeScript`, but **no simulation code anywhere calls `EngineTemplate::getEngineType()`**~~ | **REFUTED 2026-09-20.** It is **virtual slot `+0xa0`** with **nine** call sites, all through the vtable — the 2026-09-16 "exhaustive grep" counted direct `call` targets only, which a virtual call never is. The values are **bit flags**, and they gate the whole ground-vehicle drivetrain (TANK-7, TANK-9, TANK-12, TANK-13) | `vtable for EngineTemplate` `0x0872bd80`, vptr = symbol + 8 = `0x0872bd88`, slot `+0xa0` → `0x0823fd00`; field `tmpl+0x524`, written by `setEngineType` `0x0823fce0`, which rejects anything above 0x1a. Enum re-derived from the 26-entry jump table at `0x086cf7b0` used by `operator<<(ostream&, EngineType)` `0x0823ef60` (client `0x00571180`), each index matched to the case body that pushes its string: `c_ETPlane=1` (`0x0823ef78`), `c_ETCar=2` (`0x0823ef92`), `c_ETTank=6` (`0x0823ef9b`), `c_ETShip=9` (`0x0823efa4`), `c_ETRocket=0x11` (`0x0823efad`), `c_ETTorpedo=0x19` (`0x0823efb6`), everything else default. **Bit meanings fall straight out of the masks: bit0 propeller/thrust physics, bit1 ground drivetrain, bit2 differential steering, bit3 thrust rather than propeller spin, bit4 pinned throttle.** The nine sites and their masks: `0x0823e06e` / `0x0823e0de` `Engine::Engine` `&0x10` → `handleMessage(4)`; `0x0823e165` `Engine::handleUpdate` `&0x10` → pins `Engine+0x124` to 1.0; `0x0824c8a2` `feedbackLoop` `&2`; `0x0824c906` `feedbackLoop` `&4`; `0x0824c9d1` `getCurrentDifferentialRPM` `&4`; `0x0824cc10` `updatePhysics` `&1` (the gate, TANK-7); `0x0824cc80` and `0x0824d03e` `updatePhysics` `&8`; plus the console accessor `0x08245466`. Reproduce with `grep -E "call   DWORD PTR \[e..\+0xa0\]"` over the disassembly, cross-checked against the eight `push 0x8c230` sites |
| TANK-2 | Differential/skid steering is `getCurrentDifferentialRPM(side)`, called per-`EngineGrip`-wheel from `addFriction`, keyed on the wheel's side sign, reading the **same** Engine's `roll`(throttle)/`yaw`(steering) any Engine has — a car's Engine just never binds `setInputToYaw`, so its wheels all pass `side==0` and the formula degenerates to plain uniform throttle | **confirmed, byte-exact** | Client `0x0057bcb0`, every `fnstsw`/`test ah,imm` in the function independently hand-traced from the raw flag encodings: `side==0`→throttle; `side>0`(right)→`clamp(throttle×(1−1.5×yaw),−1,1)`; `side<0`(left)→`clamp(throttle×(1+1.5×yaw),−1,1)`. `side` comes from `addFriction`'s own wheel parameter (vtable+0x34); the wheel's owning Engine is found by an ancestor walk to the nearest `CID_EngineTemplate` (`0x086c2b30`) |
| TANK-3 | ~~The gear-ratio sample index is `idx = trunc(gear/numberOfGears×100)` against a **fully materialized 101-slot array**, 1.0 everywhere except indices 20/40/60/80/100, so **M3A1 = 17.5** and only `numberOfGears ∈ {1,5}` touch the curve's shape~~ | **refuted and replaced 2026-09-19.** `getCurrentRatio() = 3.5 × differential / lerp(curve[i], curve[i+1], frac)`, `idxf = gear/numberOfGears × 100`, `i = trunc(idxf)` (genuine round-toward-zero), `frac = idxf − i`. **The curve is NOT flat outside its control points.** It is an `OverTimeDistribution` whose `generateDistribution()` fills all 101 slots **piecewise-linearly** between the authored points **20→3.5, 40→2.2, 60→1.5, 80→1.1, 100→0.94**; index 0 is *not* authored, so slots 0–20 ramp from the ctor default 1.0 up to 3.5. Sherman = 4.0 and Willy = 7.0 stand; **M3A1 = 5.512, not 17.5** (`numberOfGears 4`, `differential 5`, idx 25, `curve[25] = 3.175`), its four gears 5.512 / 9.459 / 14.583 / 18.617. Every gear count gets a real ratio. **Because the curve rises over indices 0–20, any gear landing below index 20 gets a higher ratio than first-of-a-5-speed: the ladder is non-monotonic for `numberOfGears > 5`** (`nGears 8, differential 5` gives g1 = 6.83 but g2 = 5.51). And `getCurrentRatio` is **load-bearing drivetrain physics, not sound**: 5 call sites. **Confirmed; REFUTES the earlier "materialized 1.0 array" and "M3A1 = 17.5"** | The earlier pass read the ctor's `0x65`-iteration default-fill loop and the five stores and never followed the eleven `CALL`s after them. `OverTimeDistribution::OverTimeDistribution(float)` lnxded `0x081e7800` (ctor arg 1.0 at `0x0823f00b`/`0x0823f054`); `generateDistribution` `0x081e7830` hand-traced, all 41 instructions: scan forward from `lo` for the next set bit of the 128-bit mask at `+0x198` (`0x081e7848`–`0x081e7866`), then `v[j] = ((hi−j)·v[lo] + (j−lo)·v[hi]) / (hi−lo)` for `j = lo..hi` (`0x081e7880`–`0x081e78a8`), with a tail case at `0x081e78b8` that sets `v[100] = v[lo]` and fills flat when nothing above `lo` is authored (**the curve is held constant after the last authored point**; moot here, index 100 is authored on both curves). `EngineTemplate::EngineTemplate` `0x0823efc0` calls it after **every** control point — 11 calls at `0x0823f0b4/0d8/0fc/120/144/165/18a/1af/1d4/1f8` and the tail jump `0x0823f224`. Ratio store/mask pairs `0x0823f17b`/`f175`, `f1a6`/`f195`, `f1c5`/`f1bf`, `f1de`/`f1ea`, `f202`/`f214`, each index recomputed from the store offset **and** independently from the mask bit. Client twin `FUN_005094b0` (the same formula unrolled ×4, same tail case) called 11× from `FUN_005715d0` (`ESI = this+0x268`, `EDI = this+0x428`). Lerp and truncation in lnxded `getCurrentRatio` `0x0824ca70`: `fild [this+0xbc]` / `fild [tmpl+0x360]` / `fdivrp`, `fld ds:0x86d0ce0` = `00 00 60 40` = **3.5** read as bytes, `fmul [tmpl+0x364]` (differential), `fmul ds:0x86b01ac` = 100.0, `fldcw 0x0c00` + `fist`, `fmul [ecx+eax*4+0x8]`×frac + `fmul [ecx+eax*4+0x4]`×(1−frac), `faddp`, `fdivp`; client `FUN_0057bd90` the same with `_ftol` `0x00804af0`, `0x008fdfa8` = 3.5, `0x008c3e80` = 100.0. The 3.5 is the **numerator multiplier on `differential`**, not a divisor and not a curve value — it is numerically equal to `curve[20]`, which is exactly why a 5-gear first gear comes out at `differential`. Curves recomputed from the control points alone in x87 extended precision: ratio idx 0..100 by 10 = 1.000 2.250 3.500 2.850 2.200 1.850 1.500 1.300 1.100 1.020 0.940; `ratio[25] = 3.175`, `[50] = 1.850`, `[75] = 1.200`. The five callers: `feedbackLoop` `0x0824c87a`, `PhysicsEngine::updatePhysics` `0x0824cfe6` (the result multiplies a Vec3 handed to `[esi+0x68]`), `ResponsePhysics::addFriction` `0x0825c252`/`0x0825c6fc`, `AnimatedBundle::updateAnimations` `0x082665ff`. No `.con` word authors either curve: `EngineTemplate::makeScript` `0x0823f580` emits only the twelve Engine words and no rodata string matches a gear-curve setter. Ctor defaults `numberOfGears = 1` (`0x0823f018`), `differential = 10.0`, `torque = 60.0`. **Extended 2026-09-20: `setNumberOfGears` `0x0823fd10` clamps its argument to [1, 5]** (`cmp edx,0x5; jle` then `mov edx,5`; `test edx,edx; jle` then `mov edx,1`), so the `nGears 8` non-monotonic ladder this row warns about is **unreachable from any `.con`** — the warning stands for the curve's shape but not for shipped data. The five control points were independently re-derived a third time, each index recovered from the store offset **and** from the mask bit and both agreeing: `[+0x3cc] = 0x40600000` = 3.5 → slot 20 (`+0x510 \|= 0x100000`), `[+0x41c] = 0x400ccccd` = 2.2 → 40 (`+0x514 \|= 0x100`), `[+0x46c] = 0x3fc00000` = 1.5 → 60 (`+0x514 \|= 0x10000000`), `[+0x4bc] = 0x3f8ccccd` = 1.1 → 80 (`+0x518 \|= 0x10000`), `[+0x50c] = 0x3f70a3d7` = 0.94 → 100 (`+0x51c \|= 0x10`); ratio-curve base `tmpl+0x37c`, torque-curve base `tmpl+0x1bc` with `[+0x1bc]` and `[+0x34c]` both `0x3f333333` = 0.7, confirming TANK-4's endpoints. Full field map from `makeScript` `0x0823f580` string/field pairs: `+0x360` numberOfGears (int), `+0x364` differential, `+0x368` torque, `+0x36c` gearUp, `+0x370` gearDown, `+0x374` gearChangeTime, `+0x520` noPropellerEffectAtSpeed, `+0x524` engineType, `+0x528` HeatIncrement, `+0x52c` CoolingFactor, `+0x530` heatBarType, `+0x534` InverseHeatBar; the remaining ctor defaults are gearUp **0.7**, gearDown **0.3**, gearChangeTime **1.0**, noPropellerEffectAtSpeed 100.0, HeatIncrement **−1.0** |

| TANK-13 | **NEW. The load term `L` is the drivetrain's feedback, and TANK-4's torque curve is its divisor — not a multiplier on drive force.** `PhysicsEngine+0xa4`, written only by `feedbackLoop`, whose only ground-vehicle caller is `addFriction` passing the Coulomb-clamped friction velocity change and the wheel forward. `L0 = dot(v, fwd) · getCurrentRatio() / getCurrentTorque()`; `engineType & 2` (car and tank) clamps `L0` to [−1, 1] and rescales `v`; `& 4` (tank) keeps the frame **min** of `L0` when revs > 0 and the **max** when revs ≤ 0, otherwise (car) it is a running mean `L = (L·n + L0)·0.99/(n+1)`. At redline `getCurrentTorque` returns `0.70 × setTorque`, which *shrinks* the denominator and so **adds** load feedback rather than removing power. `viewer/ground.js` instead uses `engineTorqueFraction` to scale drive: same monotonic direction, different mechanism, and it should say so | **confirmed, new** | `PhysicsEngine::feedbackLoop` `0x0824c850`: `0x0824c877` dot, `0x0824c885` `fmulp` by `getCurrentRatio()`, `0x0824c89d` `fdivrp` by `getCurrentTorque()`; `&2` test `0x0824c8ab`; `&4` test `0x0824c90f` with the min/max block `0x0824c917`–`0x0824c950`; car mean `0x0824c952`–`0x0824c97e` with `0.99` = `ds:0x86d0cdc` (`a4 70 7d 3f`) and the count at `+0xac`. Caller `addFriction` `0x0825bc45`, which multiplies the same vector by `ds:0x8716b5c` = 30.0f immediately afterwards at `0x0825bc67` — `feedbackLoop` sees it **unscaled** |

## The in-game console (2026-09-19; text colour, the completion rule and three dispatch codes stay open)

The console the tilde key opens: one class in both binaries, `OldConsole`
(lnxded `dice::ref2::io::OldConsole`, ctor `0x083dbfc0`; client ctor
`0x005a2230`, vtable `0x009033a8`, object `0x918` bytes). Read for the map
viewer's `console.js`, which reproduces the reference capture line for line.
Full narrative: [subsystems/console.md](subsystems/console.md).

**`CON-1` is not part of this family.** It sits in "Other formats" and is about
`.con` *file* parsing in the extractor. The prefix is shared and the subjects
are not.

Where a row below carries a correction, the correction is a second reading of
the same code that found the first one wrong, and the corrected text is what
this table states.

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| CON-2 | The console opens on **`c_GIToggleConsole` = 1**, bound to **`IDKey_Grave` and `IDKey_Capital`**, both `c_CMNonRepetive`. **1.61 has no spawn-screen or deploy key at all** — grepping `spawn`/`deploy` over `Settings/Default/Controls/*.con` returns only the Caps Lock console line, and the engine's whole `c_GI*` set (36 names in the client, 46 in the server) contains no deploy, spawn or kit input: the spawn screen is entered by game flow — join, death — never by a keystroke. So a viewer that binds Caps Lock to a deploy screen is inventing a control the game does not have | **verified** | Ordinal recovered by walking every `addConstantHelper` site in `io::Module::init` (lnxded `0x083f7f40`), call sites `0x083f8db0`/`0x083f8dbd`. Bindings in `Mods/bf1942/Settings/Default/Controls/Common.con` lines 26–27 and in the client's own compiled fallback control map, `0x00927088` (Grave) / `0x00927020` (Capital), part of the block `0x00926c28`–`0x00927410` that mirrors that file |
| CON-3 | The console **does not animate**. Visibility is one byte, `Setup+0x2d8 + 0xe3`; showing it rebuilds the wash quad from scratch | **verified** | Setter `FUN_00464af0` (`0x00464af0`–`0x00464b18`), quad build `FUN_004649f0`; the overlay pass tests the byte at `0x00467215` and calls the drawer at `0x00467221`. Nothing on the path tweens anything |
| CON-4 | The band is **`(lineHeight + 1) · lineCount + 4` pixels**, where `lineCount` is what `getLines(20)` returned, divided by the screen height to size a full-width quad. **It is not a fixed fraction of the screen**, and it shrinks when the scrollback is short, because `getLines` does not pad | **verified** | Drawer `FUN_00464ee0`, re-derived instruction by instruction: `PUSH 0x14` at `0x00464f96` then `CALL [EAX+0x1c]` on `[0x009a9420]`; the count is `(vec.end − vec.begin) / 0x1c` via the `0x92492493` / `SAR 4` magic at `0x00464fb0`–`0x00464fc3`; `CALL [EDX+0x14]` on the text object, then `INC EAX` / `IMUL EAX,EDI` / `ADD EAX,0x4` at `0x00464fca`–`0x00464fce`; `FDIVR` by the viewport's fourth out-parameter at `0x00465020`; `setSize({1.0f, frac})` at `0x00465033`. Text layout confirmed with it: the arguments to `[EBP+0x10]` are `(x = 2.0f, y = (h+1)·i + 2, &string)` at `0x0046505b`–`0x0046507d`. No padding, in both binaries (lnxded's short path `0x083ec147` leaves the count at the deque size and the first index at 0). The reference capture's 39% is simply what 20 lines × 22 px comes to on a 1124 px screen |
| CON-5 | The wash is **`texture/white.tga` at alpha 0.8**, SRCALPHA/INVSRCALPHA. **The RGB is the texture's and the alpha rides `D3DRS_TEXTUREFACTOR`** — not a vertex colour, which is what a first reading called it | **verified; one mechanism corrected** | `FUN_004649f0` loads the texture and builds the quad at mode 2 / alpha 0.5f, then overrides: `FUN_006084c0(quad, 1)` sets blend mode 1 and `FUN_006084e0(quad, 0x3f4ccccd)` sets alpha **0.8f exactly** (at `0x00464ad6`). Mode 1 → `FUN_0045ff10(5, 6)` → `SetRenderState(0x13, 5)` / `(0x14, 6)` on `ds:0x9c0184` + 0xc8 (`0x006085e8`); the enums check out against wine's `d3d8types.h` (`D3DBLEND_SRCALPHA = 5`, `D3DBLEND_INVSRCALPHA = 6`, `D3DRS_SRCBLEND = 19 = 0x13`, `D3DRS_DESTBLEND = 20 = 0x14`). **The correction:** the `(int)(alpha·255) << 24` at `0x006085b4` goes to `FUN_005b84e0` = `SetRenderState(D3DRS_TEXTUREFACTOR = 0x3c, c)` (`0x005b84fb`/`0x005b8506`). Mode 1 then sets only the alpha stage — `FUN_004604e0(stage 0, ALPHAOP = SELECTARG1, ALPHAARG1 = D3DTA_TFACTOR, ALPHAARG2 = DIFFUSE)` — and never touches the colour stage. **The RGB really is white**: `texture/white.tga` in `texture.rfa` is an 8×8 24-bit TGA whose 192 pixel bytes are all `0xff`. The canvas reads back `[255, 255, 255, 204]` |
| CON-6 | The console draws with **`font/BF1942.font`**, the HUD's own bitmap font — not a separate face. Line `i` at `x = 2`, `y = (lineHeight + 1)·i + 2` | **verified** | `FUN_00460e40` loads it into `[this+8]` and the drawer reads the same `+8` (`0x00465056`); both are called on `Setup+0x2d8` (`0x004544d1`, `0x0044ae63`). Layout `0x00465060`–`0x0046507d` (`push 0x40000000` = 2.0f). The design note that the console font is "quite unlike the HUD font" is right about the look and wrong about the file: it is the HUD font drawn 1:1 |
| CON-7 | The error pair is `"Error " + workingFile + " (" + lineNumber + "): " + line` and `"Error " + workingFile + ": " + message`. The two spaces in the first come from an **empty `workingFile`** between a string ending in a space and one starting with one | **verified** | `executeLine` `0x083ea1b7` onward, all four literals read back verbatim: `"Error "` `0x086e38c8`, `" ("` `0x08706306`, `"): "` `0x086b9c63`, `": "` `0x086f24a2`; working file at `this+0x1cc`. Stream assembly `<< "Error " << workingFile << " (" << lineNumber << "): " << line`, output through vtable `+0x10` at `0x083ea2d8`; the second line appends `": "` and the message and outputs at `0x083ea383`. Client literals `0x008d1f6c` / `0x008d7a24` / `0x008d1fe0` / `0x008d1fe4` |
| CON-8 | The number in `Error  (N):` is `this+0x1d0`, a line counter — but **a line typed at the prompt NEVER bumps it**. The increment is behind `executeLine`'s **fifth bool**, and the key handler passes that as **false**. So at the prompt the number is a **constant** — the same N on every error of the session — and it is a `.con` file's line number only inside a file | **verified (mechanism); the consequence CORRECTED** — a first reading had it incrementing once per typed line, and a page built on that printed 0, 1, 2… | Increment `0x083e9f89`–`0x083e9f90`, past the switch at `0x083e9f5d` **and behind** `cmp BYTE PTR [ebp-0x8b5],0` at `0x083e9f7d`; `ebp-0x8b5` is the fifth bool, stored from `ebp+0x24` at `0x083e9b0d`. Client: `cmp byte ptr [ESP+0x768]` at `0x005a12a5`, `INC dword ptr [ESI+0x328]`. `updateAsciiKey`'s Enter branch pushes `0,0,1,1,1` (lnxded `0x083eabe5`, client `0x005a1381`) ⇒ **false**; `executeLines` (`0x083ea83b`) false; **only `run` passes true** (`0x083ecafd`), and `run` saves, zeroes and restores the counter (`0x083ec6d3`/`0x083ec6f1`/`0x083ecbac`), as does `include` (`0x083ed3f1`/`0x083ed415`/`0x083ed8d3`). No path was found that leaves the top-level counter non-zero, so the reference capture's `(2)` stays **UNVERIFIED** rather than explained. Settling test on a live client: type two bad commands; if both print the same number, this reading is right |
| CON-9 | The console consumes exactly four game inputs — `c_GIUp` → `0x10` and `c_GIDown` → `0x0e` (history), `c_GIPageUp` → `0x02` and `c_GIPageDown` → `0x06` (scroll, 8 lines, clamped to `[0, pages−1]`). Left and Right are bound in the default control map and **never read: there is no cursor**. Backspace drops the **last** character, Delete clears the line, Tab completes. **Any other key resets both offsets** | **verified** | `updateGameInput` `0x083eac60` (bits 3/4/12/13, pushes at `0x083ead8b`/`0x083ead78`/`0x083ead68`/`0x083ead54`); `updateAsciiKey` `0x083ea900`; page size 8 from the ctor (`0x083dc0d6`, client `param_1[0x57]`). The reset is the tail at `0x083ea973`, reached by **every** branch (Enter via `0x083eac29`, Tab via `0x083eab77`): `historyIndex *= (ch == 0x10 \|\| ch == 0x0e)` and `scroll *= (ch == 0x02 \|\| ch == 0x06)`, the two `imul`s at `0x083ea98b` and `0x083ea9a0`. Typing anything snaps both back to the live line |
| CON-10 | Scrolled up, the sixty-`+` marker is an **EXTRA row above the prompt**, not a replacement for it: `getLines(n)` returns up to `n−1` scrollback lines, then the marker when the scroll offset is non-zero, then `prompt + editLine` **on every path**. The view is 20 lines either way. It does not pad | **verified; CORRECTED** — a first reading had the marker replacing the prompt, and a page built on that lost the line being typed and one row of band height | lnxded: the marker block `0x083ec287` pushes the sixty `+` (`0x086e38e0`), and **both** its exits (`0x083ec2cd jg`, `0x083ec2df jmp`) land on `0x083ec1ff`, which `strlen`s the edit buffer at `this+0x60`, appends it to the prompt copy and pushes that — the same instruction the unscrolled path reaches from `0x083ec1f6`. Client agrees: `0x005a2037 JZ 0x005a2060` skips only the marker, and `0x005a2060` (append `this+0x38` to the prompt) is on every path. `getLines` `0x083ec0e0`, client `0x005a1fa0`; marker string client `0x00903448`; short-path jump `0x083ec147`, `dec edi` `0x083ec174` |
| CON-11 | A line splits at the **first `.`**; keywords are matched with `strcasecmp`; arguments are whitespace- or `"`-delimited. **`=` is not accepted between a method and its arguments** — `handleCommand` contains no `cmp …,0x3d` at all, and the only `=` the dispatcher understands is `var` / `const` / assignment to an existing `v_` variable | **verified, and stronger than first reported** — the whole binary compares a token against the `"="` literal `0x086e6da4` in **three** places, all `std::string::compare` inside `handleCommand`: `0x083e14c3`, **`0x083e1a43`** (missed by the first pass) and `0x083e78ce` | Dot scan `cmp BYTE PTR [edi+ebx],0x2e` at `0x083e422d`, `substr(0,i)` at `0x083e4244`; `strcasecmp` `0x083e41b1`/`0x083e41ef`; `getArgs` `0x083de4d0` tests only 0x20 and 0x22 (quote handling `0x083de8cf`), with no `=` case. Survey: **0 of 86** loose `.con`/`.inc`/`.tweak` files use `object.method = value`. A viewer may accept the form as a documented divergence — but strip it from the **raw text before tokenising**, anchored and with a lookahead, or a quoted `"="` first argument is swallowed |
| CON-12 | **`game.showHud` does not exist in 1.61.** The HUD toggle is `game.useHud` | **verified** | `strings -n 3 BF1942.exe \| grep -cxF showHud` → 0; `useHud` → 1. `Mods/bf1942/Settings/AliasedCommands.con` aliases `hud` to `game.usehud`, which works because lookup folds case |
| CON-13 | Ctor defaults: prompt `"> "`, max line **77** (the edit buffer is in-object at `this+0x60`, which is why there is no cursor), page **8**, **scrollback 1,024** and **command history 10**. The `10000` in the same constructor is a **third member nothing reads** | **verified; the two caps CORRECTED** — a first reading left the 10000/10 assignment open and a page took 10000 for both | `setMaxHistorySize` writes `this+0x2c`, which is exactly what `output` compares the **scrollback** deque against before popping (`0x083dd018`), and the ctor sets it to **0x400 = 1024** (lnxded `0x083dc037`, client `0x005a224c` `[esi+0x18]`). `setMaxCommandHistorySize` writes `this+0x5c` = **10** (lnxded `0x083dc09c`, client `0x005a226e` `[esi+0x34]`). The 10000 at `+0x1bc`/`+0x2fc` has no reader. Other ctor values: client `0x005a2230` (`param_1[0x4e] = 0x4d`, `param_1[0x57] = 8`), lnxded `0x083dc0a3`/`0x083dc0d6`/`0x083dc14c`. **`output` also wraps**: `0x083dcf50` closes a line on NUL, on `\n` (`0x083dcf9c`) **and** on `cmp ecx,[edi+0x160]` / `jle` at `0x083dcfce` — the `jle` makes the chunk **78**, not 77 |

### Still open on the console

- **The text colour.** `FUN_00460e40` sets none and the drawer's per-line call
  carries none (`x`, `y`, `&string` only), so it is a member or inherited device
  state; the text object at `Setup+0x2d8+0x08` is not a bare `Font` (its vtable
  slot 0x10 is not `Font`'s) and its vtable was not walked. Black is from the
  capture. One hypothesis, with an address and **unproven**: the wash leaves
  `D3DRS_TEXTUREFACTOR` at `0xCC000000` (RGB zero, alpha 204), so text quads
  that modulate by TFACTOR would come out black *and* ~20% transparent with no
  colour set anywhere. Sampling a glyph interior in a real capture settles it —
  flat `#000000` kills it, scene bleed-through confirms it.
- **`autoCompletion` (`0x083e83a0`)**: whether it completes to the longest
  common prefix or to the first match.
- **The history walk wraps in the engine** (`0x083eab5a` negative → size−1,
  `0x083eaa99` size → 0, no empty "fresh" slot) where a clamping viewer
  differs only at the two ends. Which end of the deque a new command is pushed
  onto was not read, so the wrap was not reproduced.
- **`handleCommand` codes 0 and 3** (`executeLine` branches `0x083ea4c3`,
  `0x083ea069`).
- **The leading `@`** in `@Adding <%s> (%d) to buddylist` (`0x008d62a7`):
  something strips it and that path was not read.

## Skeleton IK — `addSkeletonIK` (settled 2026-09-19)

Where a seated soldier's hands go. `addSkeletonIK` is a `.con` word on an
`AnimatedBundleTemplate`; the engine resolves it every frame into a two-bone
reach plus an outright rotation override. Read for the map viewer's seat poses;
full narrative: [subsystems/skeleton-ik.md](subsystems/skeleton-ik.md). Survey:
2,338 lines across 16 mods, 2,097 live, zero unparsed, always exactly two
slash-triples, and only four bone names ever occur (`R_Hand` 1,035, `L_Hand`
1,016, `L_Foot` 24, `R_Foot` 22).

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| IK-1 | The offsets are in the frame of **the child added most recently before the line**, not the declaring node's. A line before any child gets `-1` and measures from the node itself | **confirmed** | `SkeletonIkInfo` is 88 bytes (`+0x00` short bone-name id, `+0x04` int target child, `+0x08` cached bone index, `+0x0c` Vec3, `+0x18` Mat4; `add edx,0x58`). `addSkeletonIK` `0x08266cb0` fills `+0x04` from `call [eax+0x8c]` on `this`'s vptr then `dec eax` (`0x8266d98`) — vptr + 0x8c is `vtable for AnimatedBundleTemplate` + 0x94 = **`BundleTemplate::getNoTemplates()` `0x081a98f0`**, which returns `([+0x144] − [+0x140]) / 4`, and `addTemplate` `0x081a8e70` appends one entry. `updateIk` `0x08265880` loads `+0x04` at `0x82659ed`, takes the declaring node when negative (`js 0x82659f2`), else `getChild()` (vptr+0x7c) then N `+0x54` siblings (`0x8265a15`–`0x8265a1f`), then `getAbsoluteTransformation()` (vptr+0x40). Runtime child index equals template order: `addBundleChilds` `0x081a8300` walks the template vector forward and `addChild` appends onto the tail (`0x8165ea0`), skipping nothing. **Data check:** of 2,097 live lines, 1,423 have no child before them and 540 have one at the parent's origin (both readings agree); **134 across 70 templates genuinely differ**, and in every one the child reading is what lands a hand — `25mmAA_handle2_grip` puts the hand exactly on the crank, `BMG_Browning` on the spade grips instead of 1.3 m behind the gunner, and `BTR60SteeringDummy`/`zis5steeringdummy`/`GunCarrierSteeringDummy` have a child at the origin that **rotates**, so only the child reading turns the hands with the wheel. Counter-example worth knowing: bf1918's `GunboatGRohr` copies the stock Browning offset onto a template whose only child is a muzzle 1.1 m forward, and the engine really does put that gunner's hands at the muzzle — the rule is the engine's, that mod is wrong |
| IK-2 | The second triple is Refractor **yaw/pitch/roll in degrees**, baked to a Mat4 at parse time by the same helper `ObjectTemplate.setRotation` uses. **The composition is three `rotateAboutLine` calls about the matrix's own rows — intrinsic Y-X'-Z'' — not `rotateZDeg`** | **confirmed in the conclusion; one link of the first chain CORRECTED** | `0x8266d41` calls `dice::ref2::setRotation` `0x08060d30`, and `BundleTemplate::setRotation` `0x081a9050` calls the same helper at `0x081a9085` — that identity is the argument, and `ObjectTemplate.setRotation`'s arguments are unambiguously degrees (`WillySteeringDummy` is itself placed `setRotation 0/34/0`). Inside it, `yaw`/`pitch`/`roll` (`0x08061db0`/`0x08061dd0`/`0x08061df0`) each call `rotateAboutLine` `0x08061e10` about the matrix's **own row** — `m+0x10`, `m+0x00`, `m+0x20` — which is an intrinsic Y-X'-Z'' composition, equal to the extrinsic `Ry·Rx·Rz` a `quat_from_ypr` builds. `rotateZDeg` `0x080625f0` is a different helper and is **not** on this path |
| IK-3 | The solve is a **two-bone reach on bones `i−1` and `i−2`**, followed by an outright override of the hand bone's world rotation, keeping the translation the solve produced | **confirmed** | `applyIk` `0x08342610` only *records* an `IkHandle`. `Skeleton::transform` `0x083420f0` reads the handle index at `bone+0xe0`, calls `applyIK2BoneSolver` `0x083418f0` (→ `maya::applyIK2BoneSolver` `0x08332e10`) when it is not −1, and after the `mult` copies the handle's rotation rows over the bone's world matrix at `0x8342233`–`0x83422a7`. The solver builds `edx = base + 232·i` at `0x08341908` and reads `−0xa0`/`−0x90`/`−0x80` (i−1) and `−0x1d0` (i−2). **Those indices are the forearm and the upper arm**: in `UsSoldier.ske`, `Bip01 R Hand` is 44 with R Forearm 43 and R UpperArm 42; L Hand 21/20/19; L Foot 4/3/2; R Foot 8/7/6 — for all four bones the data ever names, array `i−1`/`i−2` equals parent/grandparent, so a hierarchical walk reaches the same chain |
| IK-4 | One IK entry per bone per template: a later line for the same bone **replaces** an earlier one — but **only the Vec3 and the Mat4 are rewritten. The target child keeps the first declaration's value** | **confirmed; the replace semantics CORRECTED** — a first reading replaced the whole entry, which is wrong for 24 lines across six mods (FHSW `Hotchkiss`, FH/FHSW `PT_BoatDriverHandDummy`, `AA_Allies_Crank`, `LcvpHandDummy2`, and Interstate's `stratos` / `RedLightning` / `buggy` steering dummies) | `0x8266d70` scans the existing entries by name id; the replace branch at `0x8266e1a` writes only `slot+0x0c` and `slot+0x18` and **never reaches `0x8266d98`**, the `getNoTemplates()−1` store that IK-1 describes |

## The combat area (2026-09-19; the terrain-material half is read and not modelled)

What a level's `game.setActiveCombatArea` does at run time, and what leaving it
costs. This is where level-scope rules live in this corpus; narrative in
[subsystems/combat-area.md](subsystems/combat-area.md). All addresses
`bf1942_lnxded.static`. Counted across the extracted levels: **11 of 23 vanilla
`scene.json` files carry a non-null `combatArea`** — all 23 carry the key and
twelve carry it as `null` (aberdeen, battleaxe, bocage, coral_sea, el_alamein,
gazala, guadalcanal, iwo_jima, kharkov, kursk, midway, wake); of 277 extracted
levels across all mods, 250 are non-null.

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| CA-1 | `Game.setActiveCombatArea` takes an **ORIGIN and a SIZE**, not two corners (`bf42/level.py`'s `CombatArea`) | **verified** | `Game::setActiveCombatArea` `0x08061840` stores four floats at `Game+0x70`…`+0x7c` and sets `useActiveCombatArea` (`+0x6d`). The proof is the engine's own arithmetic, not a level's numbers: `gameStatusPlaying` reads them back through `getActiveCombatArea` `0x08061870` and **adds** the third to the first and the fourth to the second to get the far corner — `0x0815237a fld` / `0x08152383 fadd` / `0x08152389 fstp` → maxX, and `0x0815238f` / `0x08152395` / `0x0815239b` → maxZ. A corner pair would never be summed. Checked against seven levels' raw `.con` values versus the extracted `scene.json`; **Tobruk is the discriminator** — `1024 0 2048 2048` is x 1024…3072 as origin+size and an innocent-looking x 1024…2048 as corners. With no area declared the same block falls through at `0x08152575` to the terrain's own `getSizeX`/`getSizeZ` (`PatchTerrain` vtable +0x0c/+0x10) |
| CA-2 | Leaving the area is a **10 s countdown and then 5 HP/s**, not a kill — and the threshold is a **strict `>`** | **verified** | `Game::setTimeAllowedOutSideWorld(u8)` `0x08061800` → `Game+0x6c`, **default 10** at `GameServer::init` `0x08131dbb` (`mov BYTE PTR [ebx+0x6c],0xa`); no `.con` verb sets it. `GameServer::setDamageForBeingOutSideWorld(float)` `0x0813dbc0` → `GameServer+0x2e8`, **default 5.0** at `0x08131db1` (`0x40a00000`); surveyed every `.con` in all 72 vanilla archives — vanilla never overrides it, only bfheroes does (120–350). The damage is `dt · [GameServer+0x2e8]` at `0x0815247b`–`0x08152480` into `GameServer::giveDamage` (vtable `+0x15c`, `0x0814b2e0`) — **a rate, not a lump at the buzzer**: 30 HP at 5 HP/s is six more seconds, sixteen in all. `jne 0x0815251a` at `0x08152437` spares the `<=` case |
| CA-3 | The out-of-area timer is a **per-player accumulator**, zeroed on re-entry and **clamped back to the allowance** after each damage frame | **verified** | `player+0x178`: `fadd` `0x0815241f`, compared against `Game+0x6c` at `0x0815242e`–`0x08152437`, zeroed on the in-bounds branch at `0x08152553`, written back to the allowance at `0x081524a8`/`0x081524ac`/`0x081524b2` — so it reads 10 forever rather than growing. A viewer that lets it grow reports a number the engine never holds |
| CA-4 | The on-screen warning is `menu/InGame` top-level entry **#42**, gated `0 < Outside/OutsideTime`, and its text is the node's own Wstring — **"Warning! You are leaving the combat area! Desserters will be shot!"**, misspelling included. It is **not** the lexicon's `DESSERTION_MESSAGE` ("…combat area. Deserters will be shot."), which that node never reads. Both strings ship; only one is on screen | **verified (from the data)** | Decoded from `menu.rfa`: plate `textmessBG_3line_256x64` at (305,171), text at (310,174) in Trebuchet MS8 coloured (0.8516, 0.3516, 0.3516), right-aligned countdown at (536,189) in Trebuchet MS11-Latin. The plate's own name is what settles the wrapping: it is a **three-line** plate, so the 65-character string is meant to wrap |
| CA-5 | ~~A **team** check at `0x08152540` gates the accumulator reset~~ | **verified, and the first reading was WRONG** — it is not a team check. The in-bounds branch asks the terrain for **the material under the player** and compares it with `GameServer::materialToGiveDamage`, **default 7**; on a **match** it jumps to the same accumulate path, and only a mismatch zeroes the accumulator. **A second, non-geometric way to be outside** — standing on a particular terrain material bleeds you exactly as leaving the box does. Not modelled anywhere: we carry no terrain material channel | In-bounds branch `0x08152525`: `dice::ref2::geom::terrainBase` `0x087435f0`, vtable **`+0x4c` = `PatchTerrain::getMaterial(float, float)` `0x083d6800`**, compared at `0x08152540` with `GameServer+0x474`. Setter `GameServer::setMaterialToGiveDamage(unsigned char)` `0x0813dff0`, getter `0x0813e020`, constructor default **7** at `0x0812f287` |
| CA-6 | The area is **inclusive on all four edges** and **never bounds altitude** | **verified** | Four `fucomp`/`fucom` + `test ah,0x45`, each branch taken only on a strict `>`: `0x081523c1` (`minX > x`), `0x081523d7` (`minZ > z`), `0x081523ec` (`x > maxX`) all jump to the outside path, and `0x08152403`'s `jne` goes to the inside branch unless `z > maxZ`. Only the position's `+0` (x) and `+8` (z) are ever loaded — a bomber orbiting at 400 m over the middle is safe; one that drifts sideways is not |
| CA-7 | **The object the player OCCUPIES is tested and damaged** — the vehicle when seated, the soldier on foot. The hull burns; the pilot inside it is untouched | **verified** | The position tested at `0x081523a1` is `BFPlayer::getVehicle()`'s (vtable `+0x3c` = `0x080560c0`, returning `BFPlayer+0x4c`). The damage goes to the same object: `0x0815243f` branches on `BFPlayer+0x6c`, the entry-point index (−1 from the constructor at `0x08050b1e`, restored by `GameServer::exitVehicle` at `0x0814e5c2`); on foot the target is `BFPlayer+0x68`, the default vehicle `setDefaultVehicle` stores (`0x08055879`), i.e. the soldier. A viewer that runs this check only while on foot does nothing in the one case the mechanic exists for |

## The parachute and free fall (2026-09-21; the drag radius and the free-fall run-off stay open)

Stepping out of a flying aircraft, read for
[`features/viewer-parachute/`](../viewer-parachute/README.md) and built the
same day. Every row re-derived by an independent reviewer; two of the stream's
eight were corrected and are recorded here in the reviewer's wording.

| # | Assumption in our code | Where | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PARA-1 | Free fall arms on **both** `getPositionalSpeed().y < -8.0` **and** `pos.y - terrainHeight > 10.0`, the height test nested inside the speed test, the whole branch skipped when an active kit part's template carries a non-zero byte at `+0x62` | [parachute.js](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/parachute.js) | **confirmed** (re-derived 2026-09-21) | `BFSoldier::handlePlayerInput` `0x08275eaa`-`0x08275f5d`; consts `0x086d2714` = `c1000000` = -8.0 and `0x086b9314` = `41200000` = 10.0, read from the file; `terrainBase` `0x087435f0` vtable `+0x54`. **`BFSoldier::triggerFallingAnimation` `0x0827e920` is not this path**: it only stamps `WorldTime` into `BFSoldier+0x584`, occupies no vtable slot, and has exactly one caller, `GameServer::handleExplosionOnObject` `0x08156bb3` - it is blast knockback. (The brief that named it as the falling trigger was wrong) |
| PARA-2 | `setParachuteSpeed` (`BFSoldierTemplate+0x2e8`) is a terminal speed, and `SOLDIER_BOUNDING_RADIUS = 0.8` follows from it | [physics.js](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/physics.js) | **refuted** (2026-09-21) | It is an **acceleration**: 30 m/s^2 along a forward axis every tick. Read in exactly two places, both `BFSoldier::handleUpdate` `0x08271e30` - `0x08272700` (free fall, the **camera's** absolute transform row 2, the product's `y` clamped to <= 0 at `0x0827274d`-`0x08272764`) and `0x082727f2` (canopy, the **soldier's** own, unclamped). `PointPhysicsNode::addAccelerationAtRelativePosition` `0x08256650` reads its **second** argument into the `+0x1c` accumulator and ignores the first; the signature is `(position, acceleration)` and the sibling `PhysicsNode::` version `0x08255230` does use arg 1 as a lever arm. Consequence that needs no radius: the terminal glide ratio is `parachuteSpeed : \|g\|` = **2.037 : 1**, measured 2.0365 on the page |
| PARA-3 | Which forward axis the parachute force uses | [parachute.js](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/parachute.js) | **confirmed** | `BFSoldier+0x3f0` is a `std::list<ICameraObject*>` sentinel - the ctor allocates it through `__simple_alloc<_List_node<ICameraObject*>>::allocate(1)` at `0x0826c1f1` and makes it circular - so `+8` is that node's data. Row 2 is forward two ways: `setRotateYDeg<float>` `0x081a24b0` writes `m[0x00]=cos, m[0x08]=-sin, m[0x20]=sin, m[0x28]=cos`, and the same `+0x20` triple is dotted against velocity to pick `Lb_ExplosionForward`/`Backward`. Clamp sense verified from the flags: `test ah,0x45` clears only for ST0 > ST1, so the store of `0.0` over `y` is taken exactly when `y > 0` |
| PARA-4 | The chute is a kit item and 9 opens it | [parachute.js](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/parachute.js) | **confirmed** | There is no `c_PIParachute`. Input bit 22 = `c_PIMenuSelect9` -> `GameServer::checkPlayerTriggers` `0x08150018` (one ladder mapping bits 14..22 onto TemplateMessage 10..18, each gated on the analog value > 0.5) -> `BFSoldier::handleMessage` `0x0827728b` (`cmp edi,0x12`), which calls `setIsParachuting(true)` at `0x08277ba7` **only** while the lower-body state is `Lb_ParachuteFall` |
| PARA-5 | The parachute's effect on physics | [physics.js](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/physics.js) | **confirmed** | `setIsParachuting(bool)` `0x08276f90`: state bit `0x10` at `BFSoldier+0x3e6`; `PointPhysicsNode::setDrag` (`[BFSoldier+0x60]`, vtable `+0x94`) with `BFSoldierTemplate+0x2e4` (`setParachuteDrag`) on open and `+0x44` (`ObjectTemplate.drag`) on close; the `Parachute` child at `+0x26c` driven to animation state `"OpenParachute"` (the `c_SstOpenParachute` literal read 5 bytes in, past the `c_Sst`); Lb/Ub clips with the `Dead*` twins chosen off byte `+0x245`. Closes at `\|vy\| <= 2.0` (`0x08272f3b`, `0x08273129`). Vanilla and 8 mods ship `drag 24 / speed 30`; FH and FHSW `24/10`; FinnWars `20/20`; GC `5000/30`; bf1918 `0/0` |
| PARA-6 | The soldier's drag bounding radius | [parachute.js](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/parachute.js) | **open**, bounded only from **above** at `r < 3.126` | **The `r >= 2.354` lower bound is REFUTED by PARA-10**: it required a chute landing to arrive under HP-14's 8.0 m/s floor to be survivable, and the engine never puts a parachutist in front of that floor at all. Only the upper bound survives - `setIsParachuting(false)` at `|vy| <= 2.0` (`0x08272f3b`, `0x08273129`) would drop the canopy in mid-air. Nothing bounds it from below, so the radius is set by **play**: 2.5 descended at about half the real game's rate (the owner, 2026-09-21), so **1.8**, where the first draft had it - 6.030 m/s down, 12.280 forward, touching down at `|v| = 13.68` and billed nothing. **The radius chain is still contradictory and this row is not closed**: `SkeletonCollisionMesh::getRadius` `0x083ad850` gives the soldier exactly 1.0, `BCompositeObject::getBoundingRadius` `0x08165630` returns 0.0 when `flags & 1`, and `setIsParachuting` **clears** that bit on open - `updateFlags(0,1)` at `0x08277027`, polarity proven from `BObject::updateFlags` `0x08164c80` computing `a & ~flags` / `b & flags` and early-exiting when both are zero, so `a` sets and `b` clears. That makes the composite ~1.0 stowed and ~13.79 open, neither flyable, so one reading in that chain is wrong. **This is the thread to pull to close PARA-6 properly** |
| PARA-7 | The bail-out sound schedule | [parachute.js](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/parachute.js) | **confirmed** | `Objects/Soldiers/Common/Sounds/SoldierFallingHigh.ssc`: layer gates from each `Volume <- Time` `Ramp p1 p1 0 1` + `trigger Volume` - `rcktlp1` (loop, vol .2) and `luft2` (loop) from 0 s, `fhs1` 1.2, `fhs2` 2.3, **`soprupp` 11.5**; a second `newPatch` with `randomPlay 1` picks one of `@Language/fallparachute{,2,3}` at 3.3. `SoldierOpenParachute.ssc` is `randomPlay 1` over `para1`@0.4 / `para2`@0.4 / `para3`@0.3. The easter egg's selection rule is a **time threshold, not a random pick**: `soprupp` is a layer of the ambience patch and wants 11.5 s of continuous falling (~830 m). **`SoldierParachuteLand.ssc` does not exist** - `SoldierSound.inc` loads it by name and both landing states declare the trigger, but no such file is in `Objects.rfa`; a chute landing is silent in retail. What `soprupp.wav` contains is UNVERIFIED (nobody listened to it) |
| PARA-8 | Fall damage on a chute landing is neutralised by resetting `lastCollisionHeight` | [soldier.js](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/soldier.js) | **refuted** (2026-09-21); the engine does no such thing | `Armor::lastCollisionHeight` (`Armor+0x28`) is written **inline at the tail of `Armor::update(float)`** `0x08172f40`, store at `0x081730b0`-`0x081730e7`, guarded by `Armor+0x129 == 0` which `Armor::collision()` `0x08174470` sets: every tick the object is **not** in contact the field is **raised** to the current `y`. It is a running maximum of altitude, reset only by contact - so `F` really is the parachutist's whole drop. `Armor::setLastCollisionHeight` `0x08174480` (vtable `+0xf8`) is a dead weak accessor with no caller; the three `+0xf8` sites in `BFSoldier` belong to another class (each tests its return with `test eax,eax`). The engine's answer to the 6,900 HP is PARA-6's radius, not this field: inside the window the touchdown is `\|v\| <= 8.0` and `handleCollisionLandOrWater` returns before `Q^2`. The viewer's re-stamp has been deleted |
| PARA-9 | Free-fall look-steering | [parachute.js](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/parachute.js) | **open** - the reading survived a **fourth** independent pass and is still not reconciled | `0x082726a8`-`0x082727cb`: `template[+0x2e8]` times rows `+0x20/+0x24/+0x28` of the **camera's** `getAbsoluteTransformation` (`vt+0x40` on `[this+0x3f0]`'s list head), added every tick through `vt+0x6c`. The **only** clamp is `0x0827274d`-`0x08272764` and it zeroes the **upward** component (`fldz / fld accel.y / fucomp / test ah,0x45 / jne / fst`); nothing clamps horizontal, `updatePositionalDragSimple` `0x08255fc0` is isotropic, `updatePositionalPhysics` `0x082560c0` zeroes the accumulator only after integrating, and `PointPhysicsNode` has **no max-speed member anywhere** in `ref2::world`. An input-gate hypothesis was proposed by the lead and is **dead**: there is no conditional between the multiply and the call and no input channel in the operand's provenance. Measured on the page: **129.8072 m/s horizontal at t = 2.0 s**. PARA-10 removes one objection (the engine is content to let a parachutist arrive at any speed) but is **not** a resolution, and no free-fall exemption was found |


| PARA-10 | **A parachute landing is billed a zero impact speed, so it is free at any speed** | [parachute.js](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/parachute.js), [soldier.js](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/soldier.js) | **confirmed** (2026-09-21; found by W5-E, independently re-derived by its reviewer) | `BFSoldier::handleCollision` `0x0827d3b0` overrides `SimpleObject`'s and forwards to it, substituting a locally built zero `Vec3` for the speed argument while state bit `0x10` is set: `0x0827d470` `mov ax,[edi+0x3e6] / and eax,0x10 / test ax,ax / je 0x0827dc60`, zeroes built `0x0827d483`-`0x0827d491`, `lea edx,[ebp-0x28]` `0x0827d494`, pushed `0x0827d4a2` with `edx` untouched in between. The not-parachuting tail `0x0827dc60` pushes `[ebp+0x10]`, the caller's real vector, into that one slot and jumps to the shared `push esi / push edi / call 0x081dab40`; **the two tails differ in nothing else**. That argument is the engine's own "speed", **read rather than inferred**: `SimpleObject::handleCollision`'s debug print `0x081dac17`-`0x081dac81` (string `0x086c8c4d`) emits `"speed:" << [ebp+0x10] << " other:" << ... << " relPos:" << [ebp+0x18]`. Slot `+0x58` is `handleCollision` on both concrete vtables (`0x0872f040+8+0x58` -> `0x0827d3b0`, `0x08725020+8+0x58` -> `0x081dab40`), which is what `PointResponsePhysics::solveImpulse` `0x08256ff7` calls with `(other, this+0x48, this+0x5c, relPos, m1, m2)`. Forwarding: `0x081daeee` -> GameServer vtable `+0x34` `0x08156020`, tail-jumping `0x08156080` to `handleCollisionLandOrWater` `0x08154960`; soldier CID `0x9493` at `0x08154a81`; `0x08155189` `fsub 8.0` then `fldz / fucomp st(1) / test ah,0x45 / je 0x08154c78`, which is the function's `ret` (polarity checked explicitly). **Free fall is NOT covered** - bit `0x10` is clear there (free fall is `0x2000`), so a man who never pulls is billed his full 101.9 m/s and dies. This, not PARA-8's height re-stamp and not PARA-6's radius, is the engine's answer to the 6,900 HP problem |

## The soldier's camera (2026-09-21; the client's view-toggle handler stays open)

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| CAM-1 | **A soldier's camera authorises exactly one view mode, `CVMInside`, and `BFSoldier::nextCamera` is an empty function** | **confirmed** (2026-09-21; the name-to-byte map upgraded from inferred to read) | `Camera::setViewMode` `0x081ac7c0` switches through the jump table `0x086c5040` (`cmp edi,0x11; ja` -> `0x081ac7f0` = `xor eax,eax; ret`); each live arm tests one byte of the camera template `[esi+0x4c]` and returns 0 when it is zero. `CameraTemplate::CameraTemplate` `0x081acc20` seeds `+0x1bc/+0x1bd/+0x1be/+0x1c1 = 1` and `+0x1bf/+0x1c0 = 0`. The word-to-byte map is **read** from the console registrations `0x081b3fdd`-`0x081b43e3`: `CVMInside`->`+0x1bc`(3), `CVMChase`->`+0x1bd`(12), `CVMFrontChase`->`+0x1c1`(13), `CVMFlyBy`->`+0x1be`(14), `CVMTrace`->`+0x1bf`(16), `CVMExternTrace`->`+0x1c0`(17). `Objects/Soldiers/Common/Objects.con` is the only vanilla file writing all six and writes `CVMInside 1` with the other five **0**. `BFSoldier::nextCamera` `0x0827f7c0` is literally `push ebp; mov ebp,esp; pop ebp; ret`; `PlayerControlObject::nextCamera` `0x083186b0` is the one that walks and wraps a list. **So the engine gives a soldier on foot no view cycling at all** |
| CAM-2 | The dedicated server never toggles a view mode, so it cannot settle what C does to a parachutist in the client | **confirmed**; the client-side question stays **UNVERIFIED** | `Camera::setViewMode` has exactly **two** call sites in the whole image (`0x081a9a4a`, `0x081a9e3a`, both inside the Camera subsystem), neither reached from an input path. `c_PIToggleCameraMode` is input channel **26** and `IDKey_C` binds it in all five shipped control maps including `Infantry.con`, where `IDKey_C` is bound to nothing else. **A withdrawn argument, recorded so nobody repeats it:** "the image holds one `shr reg,0x1a`, in `cpu_Has_SSE2`, therefore channel 26 is never read" is a **non-sequitur** - a `PlayerInput` is a 59-slot array of analog floats, not a packed bitfield (`BFSoldier::handlePlayerInput` copies it `mov eax,0x3b; rep movsd` at `0x08273cfc`; `GameServer::checkPlayerTriggers` reads channels 14-22 at `[ebp+0x48]`-`[ebp+0x68]`, four bytes apart), so a channel is never read with a shift at all. The client's channel-26 handler was not found by either pass; best lead is that W4-C put the client's `Camera::getTransformation` at `0x005659b0`, so its `setViewMode` sibling is nearby and its callers are the toggle |
| CAM-3 | `BObject::updateFlags(a, b)` is **(set, clear)**, not (clear, set) | **confirmed** | `0x08164c80` computes `a & ~flags` and `b & flags` and early-exits when both are zero - an early exit that only means "nothing changes" under the (set, clear) reading. Load-bearing for PARA-6: `setIsParachuting` pushes `0x1, 0x0` at `0x08277027`, so opening the canopy **clears** the child's bit 0 |

## Bots and the AI subsystem (2026-09-21; pathfinding and the follower AI-26..AI-32, then sensing, the contest, Fire / Scout / TakeCover, the strategic AI and spawning AI-33..AI-38, all read and built 2026-09-23; vehicles and the medic stay open)

The engine's AI, read for
[`features/bf1942-ai-research-2026-09-21/`](../bf1942-ai-research-2026-09-21/README.md).
All addresses are `bf1942_lnxded.static` unless a row says *client*. The patched
copy read has a symbol table byte-identical to the corpus's and differs in 90
bytes, none in `dice::bf::ai` (AI-23). Every row below was re-derived by an
independent second reader, who **overturned AI-4** and corrected three more.

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| AI-1 | A bot is an ordinary player: `BotMain::updatePlayerAction` `0x08526430` hands its `PlayerInput` to `AIPlayer::addInput` `0x085dcf10`, which packs a `PlayerAction` and pushes it into the **same `ActionBuffer` the network fills**. No bot-specific path exists below the input layer | **verified** | `0x085dcf10` is two calls, `PlayerAction::set` then `ActionBuffer::pushBack`; `BFPlayer+0x144` holds a *pointer to* the buffer, not the buffer inline. `GameServer::simulatePlayersUpdate` `0x0815bfa0` runs every player whose `getClient(id)` is 0 - the bot case |
| AI-2 | `PlayerInputMap` is 55 named channels registered one `addConstantHelper` call each; ch30 is `PICameraMode1`, and **there is no `PIJump`** - the engine's jump is `c_PIAction` = 9 | **verified** | `dice::ref2::io::Module::init()` `0x083f8870`, helper `0x083f7f40`, name strings from `0x086c86a5`; decoded twice by two independent scripts with identical output. Jump channel per [handweapon-view-and-deviation.md](subsystems/handweapon-view-and-deviation.md) §2 |
| AI-3 | `PlayerInput` is **55 floats at offset 0**, indexed by `PlayerInputMap`, with a 55-bit mapped-mask at `+0xdc`/`+0xe0` | **verified** | `EntryInfanteryResetControls::execute` `0x08617c40` writes `*(u32*)(pi + index*4) = 0` behind a bounds check that errors above `0x36`; `Input<PlayerInputMap,55u>::isInputMapped` `0x08056210` bound-checks `> 0x36` and shifts the `+0xdc`/`+0xe0` pair. `55 * 4 = 0xdc`, so the mask starts immediately after the array |
| AI-4 | ~~The AI runs **last** in the simulation tick, so a bot pays one tick (1/30 s) of input latency~~ | **refuted** (2026-09-21, second reader) | `updateAI` is at `+0x1b3` **by layout, not by execution**. At `0x0815c2b8` a `dec eax / je 0x0815c449` on `Game::getGameStatus` (`0x080617b0`) jumps to a cold block gcc parked at the end of the function; that block calls `updateAI` and then `jmp 0x0815c2be` at `0x0815c45b`, re-entering the body one instruction past the branch, **before** `simulatePlayersUpdate` at `0x0815c2c8`. **The AI runs first**, gated on `GameStatus == 1` (Playing, from `updateGameLogic` `0x081505c0`). A bot's action is therefore in the buffer before the consumer runs; the realised latency stays **open** because `simulatePlayerUpdate` `0x0815bd00` selects by a sequence index that was not decoded |
| AI-5 | `Game::updateAI` `0x0805da50` / `GameServer::updateAI` `0x0813a3f0` call `IAIMain::instance` (`0x0874fb8c`) slots `+0x1c` `isInitialised`, `+0x24` `tick(dt)`, `+0x28` `action()` through the secondary vptr at `vtable for AIMain` **+0x80** | **verified**, offset corrected from +0x78 | `+0x78` holds the secondary offset-to-top `0xfffffffc` and `+0x7c` the typeinfo; the thunks are at symbol `+0x9c`/`+0xa4`/`+0xa8`. Exactly the trap this file's own preamble warns about - the gcc "+8" rule applied to the primary table and forgotten for the secondary |
| AI-6 | The AI is **time-budgeted, not per-bot-per-tick**: `AIMain::action` takes `getTimeIncrease() * aiSystemQuotient` and `BotManager::action` splits `0.95x` of it into 0.40 plan-execution, `0.60*q_dec` decision, `0.60*q_path` pathfinding, `0.60*(1-q_dec-q_path)*{0.1, 0.9}` hearing/sensing | **verified** | `AIMain::action` `0x084778d0` (quotient `AIMain+0x10`, `setAISystemQuotient` `0x08477840`; components `setAISystemComponentsQuotient` `0x08477880` -> `+0x18`/`+0x20`); `BotManager::action(double const&)` `0x0849a130`; `setSystemQuotient(a,b,c)` `0x08498cd0` stores `a/(a+b+c)` and `b/(a+b+c)`. Gazala's `40 40 20` gives 0.40 / 0.24 / 0.24 / 0.012 / 0.108 |
| AI-7 | Bots are topped up **per tick** to `aiSettings.setMaxNBots`, each created as a real player and handed to `AIMain::activateBot` | **verified**; the two gating bytes are **inferred** | `GameServer::gameStatusPlaying` `0x08150df0` at `0x08150e8b`: `while (getMaxNBots() != getBotCount())` -> create player -> `IAIMain` vptr +0x10 = `AIMain::activateBot` `0x08478510` -> `addBot` `0x08477f30` -> `BotManager::addBot` `0x08499060`. The gates at `GameServer+0x64` and `+0x468` are inferred to be `game.isAiLevel` / `game.autoSpawnBots`, which reach GameServer through virtual setters |
| AI-8 | Bot names load per game mode: mode 2 runs `<level>SinglePlayer/Bots.con`, every other mode `SinglePlayer/Skirmish.con`; both only `run` the `Bf1942/Game/common/*Names.con` lists | **verified** | `GameServer::loadBots(int)` `0x08131ef0`, called from `startServer` at `0x08133582`; path literals `0x086bff2d` and `0x086bff13` |
| AI-9 | `aiTemplate.addType ITxxx` sets `AITemplate+0x08 \|= 1 << enumValue`; `InformationType` is a dense 0..29 enum | **verified** | Console record `0x087cc3c0`, handler `ConsoleClass492::executeObjectMethod` `0x08500980`. Enum re-decoded independently from the jump table `0x086f3f5c` of `operator<<(ostream&, InformationType)` `0x08485290`; `ITAir = 4`, `ITNaval = 6`, `ITSoldier = 22` |
| AI-10 | `aiMeshes` bind **by name, not by reference**: the geometry template name truncated at the **last** `_`, plus `"_a1"`, under `"AIMeshes/"`, loaded as an ordinary StandardMesh | **verified** | `AIMeshLoader::getAIMesh(IGeometry*)` `0x08479940` (`rfind(0x5f)` at `0x0847999b`, rodata `0x086f315c` = `_a1`) -> `getAIMesh(string const&)` `0x08479a10` (rodata `0x086f3160` = `AIMeshes/`). Closes the round README's "`aiMeshes.rfa` hulls" open item |
| AI-11 | Vanilla's `aiMeshes.rfa` is 180 entries (90 `.sm` + 90 `.rs`); all 90 parse, versions 9 (83) / 10 (7), and all **140 material descriptors are vertex-flags `0x411`, primitive 4** | **confirmed** (data) | Re-parsed with `bf42.stdmesh`; the `.rs` carries `materialDiffuse 0 0.7 0.3`. `AIMeshVertex::vertexFormat` `0x0874f2f8` = `0x11` belongs to the loader, **not** to the file |
| AI-12 | The shipped navigation maps live in **`Pathfinding/`**, not `AIPathfinding/`, and are **loaded, not generated**: 19 of vanilla's 23 levels ship them, and GCMOD, Pirates and interstate ship none | **verified** (loader) / **confirmed** (survey) | `AIPathfinding::loadMaps` `0x0847c580` -> `loadSearchMaps` `0x0847c5c0` builds `levelPath + "Pathfinding/"` (rodata `0x086f3198`) -> `LocalMap::loadRawFile` `0x085fefb0`. `createMaps` `0x0847b2e0` / `floodLevelZeroMap` `0x085fbae0` is the editor path. Survey reproduced row for row by the second reader. **The extractor does not know this directory exists** |
| AI-13 | A `Pathfinding/*.raw` opens with five int32 the loader refuses the file over - `p6-p5`, `p7-p5`, `p5`, `p3`, `p4` from the `CellMap` ctor - then a special-cell count and `(1<<(p6-p5))<<(p7-p5)` row records; `p5 = 6 + level` in every sample | **open** (header verified, row encoding unread) | `CellMap::loadRawFile` `0x085f86a0` validates against `CellMap+0x18/0x20/0x10/0x2c/0x24`; ctor `0x085f7af0`. Six observed headers fit exactly |
| AI-14 | Difficulty has **exactly one engine-side knob**, `aiSettings.setBotSkill`, and vanilla never sets it - the running value is `AISettings::reset()`'s default **0.75**, the game's own HARD | **verified** | `AISettings::reset()` `0x08484450` writes `+0x24 = 0x3f400000` at `0x08484613`; `getBotSkill` `0x08484760` / `setBotSkill` `0x08484770` are bare load/store, unclamped, `objectHasRange()` returns 0. Archive survey over vanilla + 18 mods: FH, FHSW, EoD, bfheroes and WarFront set 1.0, bg42 0.8, WarFront also 0.7; vanilla, XPack1/2, DC_Final, DesertCombat, GCMOD, Pirates, FinnWars and interstate never call it |
| AI-15 | `getBotSkill` has **exactly ten call sites in the whole server** - plan-step rate and trigger accuracy. It does not touch health, damage, speed, spotting or reaction | **verified by two independent enumerations** | `actionExecutePlan` `0x0849a41f` (`step = (skill*0.1 + 0.1) * getTimeIncrease()`), `ConsoleClass177` (the getter), `EntryInfoWrapper::execute` `0x08618380`, `EntryTrigger::execute` x4, `EntryTriggerContinously::execute` x3. The second reader re-did it without the first's 15-instruction heuristic: all **578** `call [reg+0x40]` sites in `.text` triaged by FPU consumption and receiver, zero direct calls to `0x08484760`, and the singleton `0x0874fe8c` never escapes into a field or a direct-call argument |
| AI-16 | Bot aim error: `dev = (1 - 0.75A)*C + F30*(F38*max(0, F34*(1-A) - (now-B)) + 0.25*(1-A))`, A = botSkill, B = `getFiringTargetTime()`, C in {0, 10, 30} | **verified** | `WeaponFireArm::setBotSkill` `0x085ee580`, x87 `0x085ee587`-`0x085ee60b` re-derived instruction by instruction by both readers; `fucom` / `test ah,0x45` / `je 0x085ee610` is the `D > 0` arm, so it is `max(0, D)` either way. Constants `[0x086ba8cc]` = 0.75, `[0x086c08ac]` = 0.25. Argument order re-derived from the push sequence `0x08625daf`-`0x08625dc9`, not assumed. Tail-jumps to `FireArms::setAIDeviation` `0x0828e350`. At the shipped 0.75: 1.5625 crosshair-radii at acquisition, 0.3125 steady, 2.5 s to settle; at 1.0 a perfect shot instantly |

The first reader had it as two tests on the target and cited `0x08625d91`, which is not an instruction boundary. Usefully, this confirms **`C = 0` for the infantry case** |
| AI-19 | `FireArms+0x190` (`aiPending`) is the bot term and nothing else - a one-shot additive contribution added into the deviation total and zeroed | **confirmed**; closes the corpus's unexplained `aiPending` channel | Writers: ctor `0x0828650f` (zero) and `setAIDeviation` `0x0828e350`/`0x0828e36e`.
